Organism: Homo sapiens
Review Status: COMPLETE
📝 Provide Detailed Feedback

Gene Description

CDC27 (also called ANAPC3 or APC3) is a tetratricopeptide-repeat (TPR) subunit of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C), the ~1.2 MDa multisubunit cullin-RING E3 ubiquitin ligase that drives the metaphase-to-anaphase transition and mitotic exit. Two copies of CDC27 form a homodimer in the TPR lobe of the complex (with CDC16/APC6, CDC23/APC8 and ANAPC7), and the CDC27 TPR superhelix carries the binding pocket for the C-terminal Ile-Arg (IR) tail of the coactivators CDC20 and CDH1/FZR1 and for the IR tail of the processivity factor ANAPC10/DOC1. CDC27 has no catalytic activity of its own: ubiquitin transfer is performed by the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 cullin-RING module with the E2 enzymes UBE2C and UBE2S, and substrate degrons are read by the coactivator WD40 domain together with ANAPC10. CDC27 instead scaffolds the ligase, anchoring the coactivator/substrate-receptor module to the catalytic core. A large disordered loop of CDC27 (residues ~180-450) is hyperphosphorylated by CDK1 and PLK1 in mitosis; the phosphorylated loop recruits Cdk-cyclin-Cks, which phosphorylates the ANAPC1 auto-inhibitory segment and thereby unmasks the C-box site so that CDC20 can bind, making CDC27 the phospho-regulatory hub through which mitotic kinases switch on APC/C-CDC20. The assembled APC/C builds K11-, K48- and branched K11/K48-linked ubiquitin chains on securin, cyclin B1, cyclin A, NEK2A and other cell-cycle regulators, triggering their proteasomal degradation, separase activation, sister-chromatid separation and CDK1 inactivation; APC/C-CDH1 then keeps mitotic regulators low from anaphase through G1. CDC27 is nuclear in interphase and localises to centrosomes and the mitotic spindle in mitosis, and injection of anti-CDC27 antibodies arrests human cells in metaphase. CDC27 is likewise required for APC/C function in meiotic divisions, and de novo CDC27 variants have been reported in hemifacial microsomia.

IPI
PMID:10548110
Accumulation of cyclin B1 requires E2F and cyclin-A-dependen... 		MODIFY
Summary: Interaction of CDC27/APC3 with the APC/C coactivator FZR1/CDH1. The cited study (abstract-only in the cache) follows reassembly of active CDH1-APC/C in S-phase cells when cyclin A/CDK2 phosphorylation of CDH1 is lost. The coactivator docks onto the APC/C through its C-terminal Ile-Arg (IR) tail, whose receptor is the TPR superhelix of CDC27/APC3, so the interaction is the scaffold contact that tethers the substrate-recruiting coactivator to the ligase.
Reason: Bare protein binding carries no functional information. The CDC27-coactivator contact is structurally defined: the coactivator IR tail inserts into a hydrophobic pocket on the APC3 TPR superhelix (PMID:26083744, PMID:27120157) and mutation of APC3 pocket residues Asn575/Leu606 impairs coactivator binding. CDC27 thereby brings the ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptor (the coactivator, GO:1990756) together with the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic module, which is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072). The replacement is the reciprocal of the anaphase-promoting complex binding (GO:0010997) used on the coactivator side. The interaction itself is not disputed.
Proposed replacements: ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity
Supporting Evidence:

Early mitotic degradation of Nek2A depends on Cdc20-independ... 		REMOVE
Summary: NEK2A, an early-mitotic APC/C substrate, binds the core APC/C directly through its C-terminal Met-Arg (MR) dipeptide tail, independently of CDC20; the authors liken this to the IR-tail docking of CDC20, CDH1 and APC10 on core subunits, and CDC27 is one of the subunits recovered.
Reason: Bare protein binding is uninformative. The biology is substrate docking on the APC/C TPR lobe, which is captured at the process level by anaphase-promoting complex-dependent catabolic process (GO:0031145); the cached abstract does not identify CDC27 as the MR-tail receptor, so no specific molecular-function replacement can be asserted. The interaction is not disputed.

PMID:17443180
Anaphase initiation is regulated by antagonistic ubiquitinat... 		REMOVE
Summary: MAD2L1 co-immunoprecipitates with CDC27 because the mitotic checkpoint complex (BUBR1-BUB3-CDC20-MAD2) binds APC/C-CDC20 and CDC27 antibodies are the standard handle for purifying the APC/C. The study concerns USP44 stabilising MAD2-CDC20 complexes.
Reason: Bare protein binding is uninformative and, for MAD2L1, does not describe a CDC27 function: MCC engagement of the APC/C is mediated through CDC20 and the APC6/APC8 TPR subunits, and depleting APC3 has no effect on MCC binding to the APC/C (PMID:21926987). CDC27's own role in this biology is captured by the APC/C membership and metaphase/anaphase transition annotations. Removal does not dispute the co-purification.

PMID:20439707
C/EBP{delta} targets cyclin D1 for proteasome-mediated degra... 		REMOVE
Summary: C/EBPdelta induces CDC27 expression, and endogenous cyclin D1 co-immunoprecipitates with CDC27; silencing CDC27 or the coactivator CDH1 raises cyclin D1 levels, so the authors propose that CDC27 directs cyclin D1 to APC/C-CDH1-dependent degradation.
Reason: Bare protein binding is uninformative. The CDC27-dependence of cyclin D1 turnover is necessity evidence for the APC/C (of which CDC27 is an essential subunit), not evidence that CDC27 itself is the cyclin D1 degron receptor: substrate recognition by the APC/C is performed by the coactivator WD40 domain with APC10 (PMID:21186364, PMID:26083744), and the silencing data implicate CDH1 equally. No specific molecular-function replacement is therefore justified; the co-immunoprecipitation itself is not disputed.
Supporting Evidence:

we detected association of endogenous cyclin D1 and Cdc27 by coimmunoprecipitation from MCF-10A cells, which was increased by proteasome inhibition with MG132

PMID:21186364
Our results suggest that substrates are recruited to the APC/C by binding to a bipartite substrate receptor composed of a coactivator protein and Doc1.

Substrate binding on the APC/C occurs between the coactivato... 		MODIFY
Summary: Interaction of CDC27/APC3 with the APC/C coactivator FZR1/CDH1. This structural study also shows that the IR tail of the processivity factor DOC1/ANAPC10 cross-links to CDC27, so CDC27 anchors both halves of the bipartite coactivator-DOC1 substrate receptor. The coactivator docks onto the APC/C through its C-terminal Ile-Arg (IR) tail, whose receptor is the TPR superhelix of CDC27/APC3, so the interaction is the scaffold contact that tethers the substrate-recruiting coactivator to the ligase.
Reason: Bare protein binding carries no functional information. The CDC27-coactivator contact is structurally defined: the coactivator IR tail inserts into a hydrophobic pocket on the APC3 TPR superhelix (PMID:26083744, PMID:27120157) and mutation of APC3 pocket residues Asn575/Leu606 impairs coactivator binding. CDC27 thereby brings the ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptor (the coactivator, GO:1990756) together with the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic module, which is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072). The replacement is the reciprocal of the anaphase-promoting complex binding (GO:0010997) used on the coactivator side. The interaction itself is not disputed.
Proposed replacements: ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity

IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: PAINT node PTN000285286 (plant, fly, yeast, trypanosome and human seeds) places APC/C membership at the APC3/CDC27 ortholog clade; human CDC27 in its own WITH/FROM reflects experimental grounding on the target. CDC27/APC3 is a core tetratricopeptide-repeat subunit of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome. Two copies form a homodimer in the TPR lobe, whose superhelix carries the IR-tail receptor for the coactivators and for APC10.
Reason: This is the defining cellular-component annotation for CDC27: cryo-EM structures of the recombinant human APC/C resolve APC3 in the TPR lobe, CDC27 antibodies are the standard handle for purifying the complex, and depleting APC3 inactivates the APC/C (stabilising cyclin B1 and delaying mitosis).

These structures define the architecture of all APC/C subunits, the position of the catalytic module

PMID:21926987
inactivating the APC/C by depleting APC3 stabilised Cyclin B1 and severely delayed mitosis

GO:0140767 enzyme-substrate adaptor activity
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		MODIFY
Summary: PAINT node PTN002649387 propagates enzyme-substrate adaptor activity to CDC27 from a single seed, human ANAPC7. Structurally, CDC27 does not bind substrate degrons: its TPR superhelix binds the IR tails of the coactivators (the true substrate adaptors) and of APC10, anchoring the substrate-receptor module to the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic module.
Reason: The IBA is treated as a considered node judgment, and the underlying biology (CDC27 as the subunit that couples substrate recruitment to the ligase) is sound, but the term is misplaced for CDC27: substrate recognition is performed by the coactivator WD40 domain with APC10 (PMID:21186364, PMID:26083744), and CDC27 provides the docking site that brings that adaptor together with the catalytic core. That is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072), which is also the term used for the ANAPC2 cullin scaffold. Whether the ANAPC7-seeded node placement is appropriate for APC3 is raised in suggested_questions.
Propagation Review
Root cause: TERM SCOPING PROBLEM
Failure modes: ROLE CONFLATION GRANULARITY MISMATCH
Sources checked:
PANTHER:PTN002649387 SUPPORTS SOURCE BUT NOT TARGET
Node seeded by human ANAPC7 experimental evidence; APC3's structurally defined partners are the coactivator and APC10 IR tails, not substrate degrons.
Proposed replacements: ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:26083744

Core Functions

CDC27/APC3 homodimer forms the coactivator-docking platform of the APC/C TPR lobe: its TPR superhelix binds the Ile-Arg tails of CDC20 and CDH1/FZR1 (the ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptors) and of ANAPC10, tethering the substrate-receptor module to the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic module so that D-box/KEN-box substrates such as securin and cyclin B are ubiquitinated and degraded at the metaphase/anaphase transition and through mitotic exit.

The disordered CDC27 loop (residues ~180-450, ~50 CDK/PLK1 sites) is hyperphosphorylated in mitosis and then recruits Cdk-cyclin-Cks, positioning the kinase to phosphorylate the ANAPC1 auto-inhibitory segment and relieve occlusion of the C-box site; this kinase-docking function of CDC27 is what converts mitotic CDK1/PLK1 activity into APC/C-CDC20 activation (no dedicated GO molecular-function term exists for this phospho-dependent kinase-docking role, so it is recorded as a contribution to ligase activation).

Suggested Questions for Experts

Q: Two GO:0005515 IPI rows (PMID:20802534, PMID:20951947) carry WITH UniProtKB:P12830 (cadherin-1, gene CDH1), but the 'Cdh1' in both papers is the APC/C coactivator FZR1 (Q9UM11). Should these WITH entities be corrected at the source (UniProt/IntAct) so that the symbol collision does not propagate?

Q: PAINT node PTN002649387 propagates enzyme-substrate adaptor activity (GO:0140767) to CDC27/APC3 from an ANAPC7 seed. Given that APC3 binds the coactivator and APC10 IR tails rather than substrate degrons, should the node carry ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072) for the APC3 branch, and does the ANAPC7 evidence support a genuine adaptor function for APC7 alone?

Q: Should the phosphorylation-dependent docking of Cdk-cyclin-Cks on the APC3 loop, which is the mechanistic step converting mitotic kinase activity into APC/C-CDC20 activation, be captured by a dedicated molecular-function annotation (currently only representable as protein kinase binding)?

Suggested Experiments

Experiment: Replace endogenous CDC27 with IR-pocket point mutants in RPE1 cells (degron-tagged endogenous allele plus rescue construct), verify APC/C integrity by APC4 co-immunoprecipitation and native PAGE, and measure CDC20/CDH1 association, cyclin B1-Venus degradation kinetics and metaphase duration by live imaging.

Hypothesis: The CDC27 IR-tail pocket (Asn575/Leu606) is required for CDC20- and CDH1-dependent APC/C activity in human cells, and pocket mutants uncouple coactivator docking from complex assembly.

Type: structure-guided mutational rescue with live-cell degradation kinetics

Experiment: Generate CDC27 loop-deletion and CDK/PLK1 phospho-site alanine alleles, quantify CDC20 versus CDH1 loading by immunoprecipitation across a synchronised cell cycle, and compare NEBD-to-anaphase timing and securin degradation in live cells with and without spindle assembly checkpoint silencing.

Hypothesis: The hyperphosphorylated CDC27 loop is the rate-limiting Cks docking site for APC/C-CDC20 activation in vivo, and its deletion or phospho-null mutation delays anaphase onset without affecting APC/C-CDH1 activity in G1.

Type: phospho-site mutagenesis with synchronised biochemistry and live imaging

The research report should be a detailed narrative explaining the function, biological processes, and localization of the gene product. Citations should be given for all claims.

You should prioritize authoritative reviews and primary scientific literature when conducting research. You can supplement
this with annotations you find in gene/protein databases, but these can be outdated or inaccurate.

We are specifically interested in the primary function of the gene - for enzymes, what reaction is catalyzed, and what is the substrate specificity? For transporters, what is the substrate? For structural proteins or adapters, what is the broader structural role? For signaling molecules, what is the role in the pathway.

We are interested in where in or outside the cell the gene product carries out its function.

We are also interested in the signaling or biochemical pathways in which the gene functions. We are less interested in broad pleiotropic effects, except where these elucidate the precise role.

Include evidence where possible. We are interested in both experimental evidence as well as inference from structure, evolution, or bioinformatic analysis. Precise studies should be prioritized over high-throughput, where available.

Human CDC27/ANAPC3 Functional-Annotation Report

Executive summary

Identity is verified. The requested protein is human cell division cycle protein 27 homolog, gene CDC27, also called ANAPC3/APC3, corresponding to UniProt P30260. The literature consistently places APC3/CDC27 in the tetratricopeptide-repeat (TPR) lobe of the human anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C), matching the supplied APC3/CDC27-family, TPR-repeat, and TPR-like helical-domain annotations. No conflicting same-symbol protein was encountered.

CDC27 is not an enzyme, transporter, secreted ligand, or membrane receptor. It is a large, intracellular, α-helical structural/regulatory scaffold within APC/C. Ubiquitin transfer is catalyzed by the APC2–APC11 cullin–RING module, whereas CDC20 or CDH1 together with APC10 supplies most substrate-degron recognition. CDC27 helps construct the TPR lobe, organize the holoenzyme, and provide interaction surfaces needed for coactivator-dependent substrate processing. Therefore, it has no independent catalytic reaction or intrinsic substrate specificity analogous to an enzyme. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3, zhou2016insightsintoapcc pages 1-2)

1. Identity, family, and domain verification

Human CDC27 is synonymous with ANAPC3/APC3. Structural literature describes APC3 as one of four canonical TPR proteins in the APC/C TPR lobe, alongside APC6, APC7, and APC8. Canonical APC/C TPR proteins are predominantly helical, contain approximately 14 TPR motifs, and form homodimeric arrangements in the complex; a reported molecular mass for human CDC27 is approximately 91.9 kDa. These observations are fully consistent with UniProt P30260 and the supplied InterPro/Pfam annotations TPR-like helical domain, TPR repeats, ANAPC3, TPR_1, and TPR_8. (chen2026thedysregulationof pages 58-61, chen2026thedysregulationof pages 19-25, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3)

The APC/C itself is a roughly 1.2–1.5-MDa, multisubunit cullin–RING E3 ubiquitin ligase. Cryo-EM resolves a triangular or bowl-like assembly containing a TPR lobe, platform, central cavity, substrate-recognition region, and peripheral catalytic module. APC3 occupies the TPR lobe, and APC7 associates specifically with APC3. This architecture supports a scaffold/organizing role rather than direct chemistry. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, chen2026thedysregulationof pages 19-25, zhou2016insightsintoapcc pages 1-2)

2. Primary molecular function

2.1 Structural and regulatory role

CDC27/APC3 helps assemble and stabilize the APC/C TPR lobe and presents interaction surfaces that organize catalytic, coactivator, and substrate-recognition modules. The best functional description is therefore APC/C structural-regulatory subunit and coactivator-docking scaffold. Its repeated helix-turn-helix TPR architecture is well suited to protein–protein interaction and assembly of large macromolecular complexes. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3, zhou2016insightsintoapcc pages 1-2)

CDC27 should not be annotated as the APC/C catalytic center. APC2 is the cullin-like subunit and APC11 contains the RING domain that recruits ubiquitin-charged E2 enzymes. CDC20 or CDH1 and APC10 form the principal degron-recognition module: the coactivator WD40 β-propeller recognizes D-box, KEN-box, and ABBA motifs, while APC10 strengthens D-box recognition. CDC27 contributes indirectly by maintaining the geometry and docking surfaces that allow these modules to operate. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, boer2016erratumtocontrolling pages 2-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3)

2.2 Complex-level reaction and substrates

Although CDC27 is non-catalytic, the APC/C containing CDC27 catalyzes transfer of ubiquitin from E2 enzymes to lysines on selected cell-cycle proteins, promoting polyubiquitylation and usually 26S-proteasome-dependent degradation. Major physiological substrates include cyclin A, cyclin B, securin, Nek2A, and numerous spindle, kinetochore, and cell-cycle regulators. Substrate selection is temporally ordered: Nek2A and cyclin A can be degraded in prometaphase, followed by securin and cyclin B around metaphase–anaphase transition. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 1-2)

Securin degradation releases separase, which cleaves cohesin and permits sister-chromatid separation. Cyclin-B destruction lowers CDK1 activity and drives mitotic exit and cytokinesis. Thus, CDC27’s broader structural role supports the irreversible proteolytic transitions underlying chromosome segregation and cell-cycle directionality. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 1-2)

3. Pathways and regulation

3.1 APC/C–CDC20 and APC/C–CDH1 phases

APC/C activity is governed mainly by interchangeable coactivators. APC/C–CDC20 operates during mitosis and promotes metaphase-to-anaphase transition and mitotic exit. APC/C–CDH1 predominates from anaphase through late mitosis and G1, maintaining low abundance of mitotic regulators and helping establish the next cell-cycle state. (chen2026thedysregulationof pages 58-61, boer2016erratumtocontrolling pages 2-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 1-2)

Human-cell interaction imaging supports dynamic reorganization of CDC27-containing APC/C. APC3–APC10 interaction increased from prophase through metaphase and declined rapidly after metaphase–anaphase transition. That study quantified ten cells from two independent experiments at each cell-cycle stage, supporting stage-dependent complex organization, although it does not establish that CDC27 alone determines substrate specificity. (liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 44-47)

3.2 Spindle-assembly checkpoint

The spindle-assembly checkpoint prevents premature APC/C–CDC20 activation while unattached or improperly attached kinetochores remain. Its mitotic checkpoint complex restrains APC/C–CDC20 until chromosomes achieve correct bipolar attachment. Checkpoint silencing then allows securin and cyclin-B degradation, separase activation, anaphase onset, and mitotic exit. Structural and biochemical evidence indicates that this inhibition involves conformational and reciprocal regulation of the holoenzyme rather than an isolated inhibitory modification of CDC27. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 1-2)

3.3 Phosphorylation

Multiple APC/C subunits are phosphorylated by mitotic kinases including CDK1 and PLK1. Holoenzyme phosphorylation promotes APC/C–CDC20 association and mitotic activation, whereas CDH1 phosphorylation prevents premature APC/C binding. CDC27 is widely used experimentally as a mitotic phosphoprotein/mobility-shift marker, but the strongest sources retrieved here support phosphorylation primarily at the APC/C-complex level; individual CDC27 phosphosites should not automatically be assigned a proven activating function. (boer2016erratumtocontrolling pages 2-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3)

4. Cellular localization

CDC27 is an intracellular APC/C component, not a membrane or extracellular protein. Its function is carried out in the nuclear/chromatin and mitotic-apparatus environment, dynamically coupled to cell-cycle stage. APC/C is associated with mitotic chromosomes and apparatus structures, where it encounters substrates such as cyclin B1.

A 2024 study using live-cell imaging, fluorescence cross-correlation spectroscopy, reconstituted biochemistry, mutagenesis, and cryo-EM found that APC/C and cyclin B1 interact primarily at the mitotic apparatus. Both can bind nucleosomes; disrupting cyclin B1’s N-terminal nucleosome-binding arginine anchor reduced APC/C interaction, delayed degradation, and produced aneuploid cells. The result supports a spatial-concentration model in which mitotic chromosomes accelerate enzyme–substrate encounter. It establishes localization of the CDC27-containing APC/C holoenzyme, but does not mean that CDC27 alone dictates all APC/C targeting. Cirillo et al., published August 2024: https://doi.org/10.1038/s44318-024-00194-2.

5. Evidence hierarchy and current understanding

The strongest evidence for CDC27’s core annotation comes from reconstituted human APC/C, cryo-EM, crystallography, native mass spectrometry, interaction biochemistry, and cell-cycle perturbation. These data directly support TPR-lobe membership and a non-catalytic scaffold/regulatory role. The inference that CDC27 itself lacks ubiquitin-transfer chemistry is especially strong because the catalytic APC2–APC11 module is spatially and structurally distinct. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3)

By contrast, many disease studies measure CDC27 expression, mutation, or splicing without demonstrating that a particular phenotype results from a defined defect in APC/C assembly or catalysis. Such associations are valuable but should be classified below direct structural and biochemical evidence. Open Targets lists CDC27 associations with several phenotypes, but the retrieved scores are modest and often derive from overlapping evidence records; they do not establish validated clinical indications or therapeutic tractability. (OpenTargets Search: -CDC27)

6. Major 2024 developments

6.1 Hemifacial microsomia: candidate developmental-gene association

Song et al. performed trio whole-exome sequencing in 12 unrelated probands and unaffected parents. Four de novo CDC27 variants occurred in three probands: p.M475R, p.F26S, p.L27P, and p.L298*. The p.M475R substitution lies in a TPR-region loop and was predicted to disrupt local structure; p.F26S and p.L27P occur in the annotated ANAPC3 region, while p.L298 is truncating. All 12 affected children had unilateral moderate-to-severe conductive hearing loss, with a mean air-conduction threshold of 80 ± 5 dBHL*. Song et al., published 26 April 2024: https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms25094707. (song2024functionalandgenetic pages 1-2, song2024functionalandgenetic pages 2-5)

CRISPR disruption of zebrafish cdc27 produced microcephaly, microphthalmia, spinal curvature, absent or hypoplastic pharyngeal cartilages, cardiac edema, disordered chondrocytes, increased apoptosis, and reduced cranial-neural-crest-cell proliferation. Injection of wild-type cdc27 mRNA rescued cartilage abnormalities; cdkn1a or tp53 disruption also produced substantial rescue, implicating inappropriate p53–CDKN1A signaling. (song2024functionalandgenetic pages 7-9, song2024functionalandgenetic pages 16-17)

This is substantial functional support for developmental relevance, but not conclusive human causality. The cohort was small, no mammalian validation was provided, heterozygous human variants were compared with a homozygous zebrafish loss-of-function model, and the authors explicitly state that disease-causing CDC27 variants had not been conclusively established. Hemifacial microsomia itself was reported to occur in approximately 1 in 3,500 to 1 in 45,000 births. (song2024functionalandgenetic pages 1-2, song2024functionalandgenetic pages 16-17)

6.2 Ovarian failure and meiotic progression

A 2024 case–control WES study analyzed 118 Spanish women with ovarian failure, 32 study controls, and public pseudo-control populations. Eighteen genes showed an excess of prioritized variants at FDR <0.05. CDC27 had nine listed SNVs, with variants reported in 104 of 118 cases, and a gene-level adjusted value of 5.35 × 10⁻⁵⁵. Pathway analyses implicated oocyte meiosis, including metaphase-to-anaphase transition. Henarejos-Castillo et al., 2024: https://doi.org/10.1186/s12958-024-01325-4. (henarejoscastillo2024wholeexomesequencingand pages 1-2, henarejoscastillo2024wholeexomesequencingand pages 5-7)

Drosophila cdc27 knockout reduced fertility and significantly decreased the proportion of mature oocytes, providing cross-species functional support. However, the work remains a candidate association rather than a clinically validated biomarker: ovarian failure is genetically heterogeneous, the human signal was based on burden analysis, and Drosophila disruption does not establish pathogenicity of each human variant. The study reports that ovarian failure affects approximately 1% of women younger than 40, that about 90% of screened cases remain idiopathic, and that genetic factors may explain 10–25% of cases. (henarejoscastillo2024wholeexomesequencingand pages 1-2, henarejoscastillo2024wholeexomesequencingand pages 5-7)

6.3 SF3B1-mutant myeloid disease and CHK1-inhibitor sensitivity

Sarchi et al. precisely engineered SF3B1-K700E in primary human CD34-positive hematopoietic stem/progenitor cells. Mutant SF3B1 caused alternative 3′ splice-site usage and reduced expression of mitotic genes, including CDC27 and BUBR1. Among 186 conserved mis-spliced genes, 34 (18.3%) were differentially expressed. Mutant cells showed delayed G2/M progression and delayed mitotic exit: they began leaving mitosis at about 90 minutes after synchronization release, compared with 60 minutes for wild-type cells. Sarchi et al., published September 2024: https://doi.org/10.1158/2643-3230.BCD-23-0230. (sarchi2024missplicingofmitotic pages 1-3, sarchi2024missplicingofmitotic pages 5-6)

CDC27 depletion or mis-splicing contributed functionally to the mitotic delay and sensitivity to CHK1 inhibition. The clinical-stage CHK1 inhibitor prexasertib preferentially depleted SF3B1-mutant immunophenotypic HSCs and impaired their engraftment in vivo. This is a promising indirect vulnerability, not evidence that prexasertib directly binds CDC27 and not yet a CDC27-targeted approved therapy. SF3B1 mutations occur in up to 30% of myelodysplastic-syndrome cases, giving the mechanism potential translational relevance. (sarchi2024missplicingofmitotic pages 1-3, sarchi2024missplicingofmitotic pages 5-6)

7. Applications and translational relevance

Current practical uses of CDC27 are predominantly research-oriented:

  1. Mitotic and APC/C activity marker. CDC27 phosphorylation-dependent mobility shifts and incorporation into APC/C are commonly used to monitor mitotic state and complex activity.
  2. Structural handle for APC/C biology. CDC27 is useful for immunoprecipitating or visualizing the APC/C, mapping subunit contacts, and studying coactivator- and checkpoint-dependent conformations.
  3. Functional-genomics readout. CDC27 depletion, knockout, or mis-splicing provides a way to test how weakened APC/C function affects chromosome segregation, development, fertility, and drug response.
  4. Candidate biomarker rather than validated clinical test. The 2024 developmental, infertility, and myeloid-neoplasm studies support further validation, but none establishes routine diagnostic or therapeutic use.
  5. Indirect therapeutic stratification. Defective CDC27 expression within broader APC/C or spliceosome dysfunction may help identify tumors vulnerable to mitotic-checkpoint or CHK1 perturbation. Direct APC/C inhibition is being explored preclinically, but systemic blockade risks harming normal proliferating tissues because APC/C is essential for chromosome segregation.

8. Expert assessment

The consensus structural interpretation is unusually clear: CDC27 is an essential architectural and regulatory APC/C subunit, not its catalytic enzyme. Assigning ubiquitin-ligase activity directly to CDC27 would be misleading. Its mechanistic importance lies in creating the TPR interaction framework through which coactivators, substrate receptors, checkpoint regulators, and catalytic subunits are coordinated. (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3, zhou2016insightsintoapcc pages 1-2)

The most important conceptual advance in 2024 was that APC/C function is controlled not only by phosphorylation, coactivators, degrons, and checkpoint inhibition, but also by spatial organization on mitotic chromatin. Recent human genetics further suggests that partial CDC27 dysfunction may manifest in development or fertility, while acquired CDC27 mis-splicing can create drug vulnerabilities in malignant hematopoiesis. These disease links are biologically plausible because proliferating neural-crest cells, meiotic oocytes, and hematopoietic progenitors are highly dependent on accurate cell-cycle transitions; nevertheless, most remain candidate or preclinical findings requiring replication.

9. Consolidated evidence table

Topic Best-supported conclusion Evidence type Key quantitative detail Source with year and DOI URL
Identity and domain architecture Human CDC27 is ANAPC3/APC3, the protein corresponding to UniProt P30260. It is a canonical, predominantly α-helical tetratricopeptide-repeat (TPR) subunit of APC/C; this agrees with the supplied APC3/CDC27-family and TPR-domain annotations. Curated identity plus structural synthesis from crystallography, mass spectrometry, and cryo-EM Approximately 91.9 kDa; canonical APC/C TPR proteins contain 14 TPR motifs and self-associate as homodimers. Alfieri, Zhang & Barford, 2017. https://doi.org/10.1098/rsob.170204 (chen2026thedysregulationof pages 58-61, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3)
Structural role in APC/C CDC27/APC3 is a core scaffolding and regulatory component of the bowl-shaped TPR lobe. It helps organize the holoenzyme and provides interaction surfaces; APC7 associates specifically with APC3. Human APC/C cryo-EM structures and authoritative structural review Metazoan APC/C contains approximately 14 core subunits and is roughly 1.2–1.5 MDa; APC3 is one of four canonical TPR-lobe proteins. Chang et al., 2014. https://doi.org/10.1038/nature13543; Alfieri et al., 2017. https://doi.org/10.1098/rsob.170204 (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, chen2026thedysregulationof pages 19-25, zhou2016insightsintoapcc pages 1-2)
Catalytic status CDC27 is not an enzyme and does not itself transfer ubiquitin. APC/C catalytic activity resides in the peripheral APC2–APC11 cullin–RING module; CDC27 supplies structural/regulatory architecture. Therefore, no CDC27-specific catalytic reaction or substrate specificity should be assigned. Structural assignment of catalytic and non-catalytic subunits Catalytic core: APC2 plus APC11; CDC27 belongs to the TPR lobe rather than this core. Alfieri et al., 2017. https://doi.org/10.1098/rsob.170204; Zhou et al., 2016. https://doi.org/10.1186/s13008-016-0021-6 (alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 4-4, zhou2016insightsintoapcc pages 1-2)
Substrate and coactivator mechanism APC/C substrate selection is chiefly performed by interchangeable coactivators CDC20 or CDH1 together with APC10, not by CDC27 alone. Coactivator WD40 domains recognize D-box, KEN-box, and ABBA degrons; APC10 strengthens D-box recognition, while the TPR lobe—including APC3—supports coactivator docking and complex organization. Cryo-EM, biochemistry, and structural review Human-cell interaction imaging used 10 cells from two experiments per mitotic stage; APC3–APC10 association increased from prophase through metaphase and fell after metaphase–anaphase transition. Alfieri et al., 2017. https://doi.org/10.1098/rsob.170204; Liu, 2020 (liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 44-47, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3)
Cell-cycle timing and checkpoint APC/C–CDC20 promotes metaphase-to-anaphase transition by degrading securin and cyclin B, enabling separase-dependent cohesin cleavage and mitotic exit; APC/C–CDH1 acts later through mitotic exit and G1. The spindle-assembly checkpoint restrains APC/C–CDC20 through the mitotic checkpoint complex until chromosomes are properly attached. APC/C phosphorylation by CDK1 and PLK1 contributes to mitotic activation, although these are holoenzyme-level mechanisms and not all can be attributed specifically to CDC27. Cell-cycle biochemistry, checkpoint structures, and reviews Temporal substrate order includes Nek2A/cyclin A in prometaphase, followed by securin/cyclin B at metaphase. Yamaguchi et al., 2016. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.molcel.2016.07.003; Alfieri et al., 2017. https://doi.org/10.1098/rsob.170204 (boer2016erratumtocontrolling pages 2-4, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 2-3, alfieri2017visualizingthecomplex pages 1-2)
Localization and spatial regulation CDC27 functions intracellularly as assembled APC/C, principally in the nucleus/chromatin–mitotic-apparatus environment during mitosis rather than as a secreted or membrane protein. Recent work showed that chromosomes spatially concentrate APC/C and cyclin B1 through nucleosome binding, accelerating substrate encounter and degradation; this establishes APC/C spatial regulation but should not be interpreted as proof that CDC27 alone dictates all localization. Live-cell imaging, fluorescence cross-correlation spectroscopy, reconstituted biochemistry, mutagenesis, and cryo-EM Mutation of cyclin B1’s nucleosome-binding arginine anchor delayed degradation and produced aneuploid cells. Cirillo et al., published 22 August 2024. https://doi.org/10.1038/s44318-024-00194-2
Hemifacial microsomia Candidate developmental-disease association, not established clinical causality. Trio sequencing found de novo CDC27 variants, while zebrafish knockout caused craniofacial cartilage defects, reduced neural-crest-cell proliferation, apoptosis, and p53-pathway activation. Rescue by cdc27 mRNA and by cdkn1a or tp53 disruption supports biological relevance, but the small cohort and species/genotype differences limit causal interpretation. Human trio-WES plus CRISPR zebrafish loss-of-function and rescue experiments 12 trios; four de novo variants in 3 unrelated probands: p.M475R, p.F26S, p.L27P, and p.L298. HFM incidence was reported as 1/3,500–1/45,000 births*. Song et al., published 26 April 2024. https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms25094707 (song2024functionalandgenetic pages 1-2, song2024functionalandgenetic pages 2-5, song2024functionalandgenetic pages 7-9, song2024functionalandgenetic pages 16-17)
Ovarian failure Candidate genetic association with preclinical functional support, not a validated diagnostic biomarker. CDC27 had an excess burden of prioritized heterozygous variants in ovarian-failure cases; Drosophila cdc27 knockout reduced oocyte maturation and fertility, consistent with APC/C function in meiotic metaphase–anaphase progression. Case–control WES burden analysis plus Drosophila knockout 118 cases, 32 study controls, and public pseudo-controls; 9 CDC27 SNVs were listed, with variants present in 104/118 cases and gene-level adjusted P reported as 5.35 × 10⁻⁵⁵. Eighteen genes and 29 subpathways met FDR <0.05. Henarejos-Castillo et al., published 2024. https://doi.org/10.1186/s12958-024-01325-4 (henarejoscastillo2024wholeexomesequencingand pages 1-2, henarejoscastillo2024wholeexomesequencingand pages 5-7)
SF3B1-mutant hematopoietic stem/progenitor cells Mechanistic preclinical vulnerability, not an approved CDC27-targeted therapy. Oncogenic SF3B1-K700E caused aberrant CDC27 splicing and lower expression; CDC27 depletion reproduced delayed G2/M transit and contributed to sensitivity to CHK1 inhibition. Prexasertib selectively depleted SF3B1-mutant HSCs and impaired engraftment, supporting indirect therapeutic exploitation of defective mitotic control. Precise editing of primary human CD34⁺ HSPCs, bulk/single-cell transcriptomics, knockdown, cell-cycle assays, drug testing, and in-vivo engraftment Among 186 conserved mis-spliced genes, 34 (18.3%) were differentially expressed. Mutant cells began exiting mitosis at about 90 min, versus 60 min for wild type. SF3B1 mutations occur in up to 30% of MDS. Sarchi et al., published September 2024. https://doi.org/10.1158/2643-3230.BCD-23-0230 (sarchi2024missplicingofmitotic pages 1-3, sarchi2024missplicingofmitotic pages 5-6)

Table: Compact evidence map for verified human CDC27/ANAPC3 (UniProt P30260), spanning molecular architecture, APC/C mechanism, localization, and major 2024 findings. Disease links are explicitly separated from established clinical causality.

Conclusion

Human CDC27/P30260 is a TPR-rich, non-catalytic APC/C scaffold that operates intracellularly within the nuclear/chromatin–mitotic apparatus. By organizing the APC/C TPR lobe and supporting coactivator-dependent substrate processing, it enables ordered ubiquitylation of securin, mitotic cyclins, and other regulators, thereby controlling anaphase onset, chromosome segregation, mitotic exit, and G1 maintenance. Cryo-EM and biochemical evidence strongly establish this core function. The leading 2024 developments concern spatial concentration of APC/C substrates on mitotic chromatin, candidate CDC27 variants in craniofacial development and ovarian failure, and CDC27 mis-splicing as part of a CHK1-inhibitor vulnerability in SF3B1-mutant hematopoietic cells. None yet constitutes an approved CDC27-specific diagnostic or therapy.

📚 Additional Documentation

Notes

(CDC27-notes.md)

CDC27 (APC3 / ANAPC3, UniProt P30260) — curation notes

Working journal for the GO annotation review of human CDC27. Citations are given inline as
[PMID:NNN "verbatim text"] and refer to the cached publications/PMID_NNN.md files. Many cached
entries are abstract-only (PMID:7736578, 16364912, 18485873, 29033132, 15678131, 18445686 and most
of the protein-binding sources); full text is cached for PMID:26083744, 27120157, 21926987,
21241890, 18662541, 21186364, 24781523, 25383541, 20439707, 20802534 and the interactome papers.

1. What CDC27 is

  • Human homolog of budding-yeast CDC27, first cloned by Tugendreich et al. and Chen et al. (UniProt
    RN[1], RN[2]); 824 aa, ~92 kDa, roughly 14 TPR motifs; belongs to the APC3/CDC27 family
    (CDC27-uniprot.txt, Pfam PF12895 ANAPC3, IPR011990/IPR019734 TPR).
  • Homodimeric TPR subunit of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome. UniProt: "Homodimer
    (PubMed:27120157, PubMed:27509861). Component of the anaphase promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C),
    composed of ANAPC1, ANAPC2, CDC27/ANAPC3, ANAPC4, ANAPC5, CDC16/ANAPC6, ANAPC7, CDC23/ANAPC8,
    ANAPC10, ANAPC11, CDC26/ANAPC12, ANAPC13, ANAPC15 and ANAPC16".
  • The APC/C is a RING E3: PMID:26083744. Catalysis is performed
    by the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 module: PMID:16364912. CDC27 is not catalytic
    (deep research: "CDC27 should not be annotated as the APC/C catalytic center").

2. Molecular function: coactivator/APC10 IR-tail receptor = ligase scaffold

  • The coactivator IR tail binds the APC3 TPR superhelix: PMID:26083744; PMID:26083744. The same is true of CDC20: PMID:27120157.
  • The second protomer of the APC3 homodimer binds the IR tail of APC10/DOC1, the other half of the
    bipartite substrate receptor: PMID:26083744; PMID:21186364; PMID:21186364.
  • The APC/C inhibitor TAME works by occupying this pocket: PMID:26083744; the TAME
    paper itself is the source of two GOA protein-binding rows PMID:20951947.
  • Conclusion: the informative MF is GO:0160072 ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity ("brings
    together an ubiquitin ligase and an ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptor"); the coactivators are the
    adaptors (GO:1990756) and ANAPC2/ANAPC11 the ligase. This mirrors the ANAPC2 review, which uses
    GO:0160072 for the cullin scaffold, and the CDC20/FZR1 reviews, which resolve the reciprocal rows to
    GO:0010997 anaphase-promoting complex binding.

3. Phospho-regulation: the APC3 loop as the mitotic-kinase docking hub

  • PMID:27120157; PMID:27120157; PMID:27120157.
  • Mechanism: PMID:27120157; PMID:27120157.
  • UniProt PTM line: "Phosphorylation on Ser-426 and Thr-446 occurs specifically during mitosis"
    (Kraft et al. 2003, PMID:14657031, not cached).
  • No GO MF term captures "phospho-dependent kinase-docking loop"; recorded as a core function that
    contributes to ligase activation, with a suggested question rather than a NEW annotation.

4. Processes

  • Loss of function: PMID:7736578; PMID:21926987.
  • Chain types built by the APC/C: PMID:18485873; PMID:29033132.
  • Meiosis: APC/C has essential meiotic functions PMID:16364912; deep research reports Drosophila cdc27 knockout reducing oocyte maturation and a CDC27 variant
    burden in human ovarian failure (candidate association). Kept as non-core for the human somatic review.

5. Localisation

  • PMID:7736578 -> centrosome, mitotic spindle (IDA).
  • UniProt: "SUBCELLULAR LOCATION: Nucleus {ECO:0000269|PubMed:18445686}. Cytoplasm, cytoskeleton,
    spindle". PMID:18445686 is the EML3 proteomic screen of nuclear microtubule-binding proteins
    (abstract-only; CDC27 not named in the abstract) - accepted, deferring to the curator/UniProt.
  • Nuclear interphase APC/C-CDH1: PMID:21241890.
  • Reactome nucleoplasm rows = APC/C-CDH1 G1 reactions; cytosol rows = mitotic APC/C-CDC20/MCC reactions.
    Both accepted as genuine compartments of the scaffold function.

6. Decisions on the 46 GO:0005515 protein-binding IPI rows

Policy (CLAUDE.md / annotation-reviewer skill): resolve bare protein binding to an informative MF or
complex membership where the evidence supports it; otherwise REMOVE as uninformative without asserting
the interaction is false.

7. IBA rows

  • PTN000285286 (PTHR12558 APC3 clade): GO:0005680, GO:0005737, GO:0007091, GO:0016567, GO:0031145 -
    all ACCEPT; human CDC27 in its own WITH/FROM is the expected marker of experimental grounding.
  • PTN001757014 (deeper node, worm seeds): GO:0051301 cell division - ACCEPT as coarse but correct.
  • PTN002649387 (seed ANAPC7 only): GO:0140767 enzyme-substrate adaptor activity - MODIFY -> GO:0160072.
    APC3 binds the adaptors (coactivator, APC10 IR tails), not substrate degrons; raised as a question
    about the node placement rather than removed.

8. Open items

  • WITH-entity error (P12830 vs Q9UM11) on two rows should be fixed upstream.
  • Whether GO needs an MF for the phospho-loop/Cks docking role.
  • Hemifacial-microsomia de novo variants (Song et al. 2024, deep research) and SF3B1-mutant mis-splicing
    are candidate disease links, not annotation-grade evidence.

id: P30260
gene_symbol: CDC27
product_type: PROTEIN
status: COMPLETE
taxon:
  id: NCBITaxon:9606
  label: Homo sapiens
description: >-
  CDC27 (also called ANAPC3 or APC3) is a tetratricopeptide-repeat (TPR) subunit of the anaphase-promoting
  complex/cyclosome (APC/C), the ~1.2 MDa multisubunit cullin-RING E3 ubiquitin ligase that drives the
  metaphase-to-anaphase transition and mitotic exit. Two copies of CDC27 form a homodimer in the TPR lobe
  of the complex (with CDC16/APC6, CDC23/APC8 and ANAPC7), and the CDC27 TPR superhelix carries the binding
  pocket for the C-terminal Ile-Arg (IR) tail of the coactivators CDC20 and CDH1/FZR1 and for the IR tail
  of the processivity factor ANAPC10/DOC1. CDC27 has no catalytic activity of its own: ubiquitin transfer
  is performed by the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 cullin-RING module with the E2 enzymes UBE2C and UBE2S, and substrate
  degrons are read by the coactivator WD40 domain together with ANAPC10. CDC27 instead scaffolds the ligase,
  anchoring the coactivator/substrate-receptor module to the catalytic core. A large disordered loop of
  CDC27 (residues ~180-450) is hyperphosphorylated by CDK1 and PLK1 in mitosis; the phosphorylated loop
  recruits Cdk-cyclin-Cks, which phosphorylates the ANAPC1 auto-inhibitory segment and thereby unmasks
  the C-box site so that CDC20 can bind, making CDC27 the phospho-regulatory hub through which mitotic
  kinases switch on APC/C-CDC20. The assembled APC/C builds K11-, K48- and branched K11/K48-linked ubiquitin
  chains on securin, cyclin B1, cyclin A, NEK2A and other cell-cycle regulators, triggering their proteasomal
  degradation, separase activation, sister-chromatid separation and CDK1 inactivation; APC/C-CDH1 then
  keeps mitotic regulators low from anaphase through G1. CDC27 is nuclear in interphase and localises
  to centrosomes and the mitotic spindle in mitosis, and injection of anti-CDC27 antibodies arrests human
  cells in metaphase. CDC27 is likewise required for APC/C function in meiotic divisions, and de novo
  CDC27 variants have been reported in hemifacial microsomia.

- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:10548110
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q9UM11
  review:
    summary: >-
      Interaction of CDC27/APC3 with the APC/C coactivator FZR1/CDH1. The cited study (abstract-only in
      the cache) follows reassembly of active CDH1-APC/C in S-phase cells when cyclin A/CDK2 phosphorylation
      of CDH1 is lost. The coactivator docks onto the APC/C through its C-terminal Ile-Arg (IR) tail,
      whose receptor is the TPR superhelix of CDC27/APC3, so the interaction is the scaffold contact that
      tethers the substrate-recruiting coactivator to the ligase.
    action: MODIFY
    reason: >-
      Bare protein binding carries no functional information. The CDC27-coactivator contact is structurally
      defined: the coactivator IR tail inserts into a hydrophobic pocket on the APC3 TPR superhelix (PMID:26083744,
      PMID:27120157) and mutation of APC3 pocket residues Asn575/Leu606 impairs coactivator binding. CDC27
      thereby brings the ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptor (the coactivator, GO:1990756) together with
      the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic module, which is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold
      activity (GO:0160072). The replacement is the reciprocal of the anaphase-promoting complex binding
      (GO:0010997) used on the coactivator side. The interaction itself is not disputed.
    proposed_replacement_terms:
    - id: GO:0160072
      label: ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity
    additional_reference_ids:
    - PMID:26083744
    - PMID:27120157
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:10548110
      supporting_text: >-
        the destruction of cyclin B1 through the re-assembly of APC, the ubiquitin ligase that is essential
        for mitotic cyclin proteolysis, with its activatory subunit Cdh1
    - reference_id: PMID:26083744
      supporting_text: The adjacent Ile of the IR tail packs into a hydrophobic pocket on Apc3.
    - reference_id: PMID:26083744
      supporting_text: Disruption of Apc3 residues Asn575 and Leu606 impaired coactivator binding
    - reference_id: PMID:27120157
      supporting_text: >-
        Nonetheless, the crucial Ile-Arg interaction of Cdc20IR with the TPR superhelix of Apc3A is conserved
        between the two coactivators

      MAD2L1 co-immunoprecipitates with CDC27 because the mitotic checkpoint complex (BUBR1-BUB3-CDC20-MAD2)

      effect on MCC binding to the APC/C (PMID:21926987). CDC27's own role in this biology is captured
      by the APC/C membership and metaphase/anaphase transition annotations. Removal does not dispute
      the co-purification.

- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:20439707
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:P24385
  review:
    summary: >-
      C/EBPdelta induces CDC27 expression, and endogenous cyclin D1 co-immunoprecipitates with CDC27;
      silencing CDC27 or the coactivator CDH1 raises cyclin D1 levels, so the authors propose that CDC27
      directs cyclin D1 to APC/C-CDH1-dependent degradation.
    action: REMOVE
    reason: >-
      Bare protein binding is uninformative. The CDC27-dependence of cyclin D1 turnover is necessity evidence
      for the APC/C (of which CDC27 is an essential subunit), not evidence that CDC27 itself is the cyclin
      D1 degron receptor: substrate recognition by the APC/C is performed by the coactivator WD40 domain
      with APC10 (PMID:21186364, PMID:26083744), and the silencing data implicate CDH1 equally. No specific
      molecular-function replacement is therefore justified; the co-immunoprecipitation itself is not
      disputed.
    additional_reference_ids:
    - PMID:21186364
    supported_by:

      supporting_text: >-

        cells, which was increased by proteasome inhibition with MG132
    - reference_id: PMID:20439707
      supporting_text: >-
        Silencing of C/EBPdelta, Cdc27, or the APC/C coactivator Cdh1 (FZR1) in MCF-10A breast epithelial
        cells increased cyclin D1 protein expression.
    - reference_id: PMID:21186364
      supporting_text: >-
        Our results suggest that substrates are recruited to the APC/C by binding to a bipartite substrate

  review:
    summary: >-
      The WITH entity of this row is UniProtKB:P12830, cadherin-1 (E-cadherin, gene CDH1), but the 'Cdh1'
      studied in the cited paper is the APC/C coactivator Cdh1/FZR1 (UniProtKB:Q9UM11): the abstract explicitly
      calls Cdh1 an activator of the anaphase-promoting complex. The paper identifies pVHL as an APC/C-CDH1
      substrate; APC/C was immunodepleted from G1 extracts with anti-CDC27, which co-depleted CDH1.
    action: MODIFY
    reason: >-
      The interaction is real but the partner identifier is a gene-symbol collision (Cdh1 the APC/C coactivator
      versus CDH1 cadherin-1); the WITH field should be Q9UM11. Bare protein binding carries no functional
      information. The CDC27-coactivator contact is structurally defined: the coactivator IR tail inserts
      into a hydrophobic pocket on the APC3 TPR superhelix (PMID:26083744, PMID:27120157) and mutation
      of APC3 pocket residues Asn575/Leu606 impairs coactivator binding. CDC27 thereby brings the ubiquitin
      ligase-substrate adaptor (the coactivator, GO:1990756) together with the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic
      module, which is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072). The
      replacement is the reciprocal of the anaphase-promoting complex binding (GO:0010997) used on the
      coactivator side. The interaction itself is not disputed.
    proposed_replacement_terms:
    - id: GO:0160072
      label: ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity

  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:22014574
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q9UM11
  review:
    summary: >-
      Interaction of CDC27/APC3 with the APC/C coactivator FZR1/CDH1. Here SIRT2 deacetylation of the
      coactivators modulates their association with the APC/C. The coactivator docks onto the APC/C through
      its C-terminal Ile-Arg (IR) tail, whose receptor is the TPR superhelix of CDC27/APC3, so the interaction
      is the scaffold contact that tethers the substrate-recruiting coactivator to the ligase.
    action: MODIFY
    reason: >-
      Bare protein binding carries no functional information. The CDC27-coactivator contact is structurally
      defined: the coactivator IR tail inserts into a hydrophobic pocket on the APC3 TPR superhelix (PMID:26083744,
      PMID:27120157) and mutation of APC3 pocket residues Asn575/Leu606 impairs coactivator binding. CDC27
      thereby brings the ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptor (the coactivator, GO:1990756) together with
      the ANAPC2-ANAPC11 catalytic module, which is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold
      activity (GO:0160072). The replacement is the reciprocal of the anaphase-promoting complex binding
      (GO:0010997) used on the coactivator side. The interaction itself is not disputed.
    proposed_replacement_terms:
    - id: GO:0160072
      label: ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity
    additional_reference_ids:
    - PMID:26083744
    - PMID:27120157
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:22014574
      supporting_text: >-
        SIRT2 regulates the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome activity through deacetylation of its
        coactivators, APC(CDH1) and CDC20
    - reference_id: PMID:26083744
      supporting_text: The adjacent Ile of the IR tail packs into a hydrophobic pocket on Apc3.
    - reference_id: PMID:26083744
      supporting_text: Disruption of Apc3 residues Asn575 and Leu606 impaired coactivator binding
    - reference_id: PMID:27120157
      supporting_text: >-
        Nonetheless, the crucial Ile-Arg interaction of Cdc20IR with the TPR superhelix of Apc3A is conserved
        between the two coactivators

      cyclin A/CDK2-CDH1 events) in the nucleoplasm, where the interphase APC/C-CDH1 acts from anaphase
      through G1.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: >-
      The APC/C is nuclear in interphase (immunofluorescence, HPA and UniProt) and its CDH1-dependent
      reactions on nuclear substrates occur there; nucleoplasm is therefore a genuine compartment of CDC27's
      scaffold function within the complex, complementary to the mitotic cytosol/spindle pool.

      on the target. CDC27/APC3 is a core tetratricopeptide-repeat subunit of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome.

  review:
    summary: >-
      PAINT node PTN002649387 propagates enzyme-substrate adaptor activity to CDC27 from a single seed,
      human ANAPC7. Structurally, CDC27 does not bind substrate degrons: its TPR superhelix binds the

    action: MODIFY
    reason: >-
      The IBA is treated as a considered node judgment, and the underlying biology (CDC27 as the subunit
      that couples substrate recruitment to the ligase) is sound, but the term is misplaced for CDC27:
      substrate recognition is performed by the coactivator WD40 domain with APC10 (PMID:21186364, PMID:26083744),
      and CDC27 provides the docking site that brings that adaptor together with the catalytic core. That
      is the definition of ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072), which is also the
      term used for the ANAPC2 cullin scaffold. Whether the ANAPC7-seeded node placement is appropriate
      for APC3 is raised in suggested_questions.

    binds the Ile-Arg tails of CDC20 and CDH1/FZR1 (the ubiquitin ligase-substrate adaptors) and of ANAPC10,

    supporting_text: >-
      engineered a bispecific antibody to detect K11/K48-linked chains and identified mitotic regulators,
      misfolded nascent polypeptides, and pathological Huntingtin variants as their endogenous substrates.
  - reference_id: PMID:16364912
    supporting_text: >-
      bound substrates are ubiquitinated by E2 enzymes that interact with a hetero-dimer of the RING subunit
      Apc11 and the cullin Apc2
  - reference_id: PMID:21926987

proposed_new_terms: []
suggested_questions:
- question: >-
    Two GO:0005515 IPI rows (PMID:20802534, PMID:20951947) carry WITH UniProtKB:P12830 (cadherin-1, gene
    CDH1), but the 'Cdh1' in both papers is the APC/C coactivator FZR1 (Q9UM11). Should these WITH entities
    be corrected at the source (UniProt/IntAct) so that the symbol collision does not propagate?
- question: >-
    PAINT node PTN002649387 propagates enzyme-substrate adaptor activity (GO:0140767) to CDC27/APC3 from
    an ANAPC7 seed. Given that APC3 binds the coactivator and APC10 IR tails rather than substrate degrons,
    should the node carry ubiquitin ligase complex scaffold activity (GO:0160072) for the APC3 branch,
    and does the ANAPC7 evidence support a genuine adaptor function for APC7 alone?
- question: >-
    Should the phosphorylation-dependent docking of Cdk-cyclin-Cks on the APC3 loop, which is the mechanistic
    step converting mitotic kinase activity into APC/C-CDC20 activation, be captured by a dedicated molecular-function
    annotation (currently only representable as protein kinase binding)?
suggested_experiments:
- hypothesis: >-
    The CDC27 IR-tail pocket (Asn575/Leu606) is required for CDC20- and CDH1-dependent APC/C activity
    in human cells, and pocket mutants uncouple coactivator docking from complex assembly.
  description: >-
    Replace endogenous CDC27 with IR-pocket point mutants in RPE1 cells (degron-tagged endogenous allele
    plus rescue construct), verify APC/C integrity by APC4 co-immunoprecipitation and native PAGE, and
    measure CDC20/CDH1 association, cyclin B1-Venus degradation kinetics and metaphase duration by live
    imaging.
  experiment_type: structure-guided mutational rescue with live-cell degradation kinetics
- hypothesis: >-
    The hyperphosphorylated CDC27 loop is the rate-limiting Cks docking site for APC/C-CDC20 activation
    in vivo, and its deletion or phospho-null mutation delays anaphase onset without affecting APC/C-CDH1
    activity in G1.
  description: >-
    Generate CDC27 loop-deletion and CDK/PLK1 phospho-site alanine alleles, quantify CDC20 versus CDH1
    loading by immunoprecipitation across a synchronised cell cycle, and compare NEBD-to-anaphase timing
    and securin degradation in live cells with and without spindle assembly checkpoint silencing.
  experiment_type: phospho-site mutagenesis with synchronised biochemistry and live imaging
